# SDK creation seams — closing the port's gaps safely (PROPOSED 2026-10-30; not go)

Source: the port's readiness list (`briefs/mundi-readiness.md`, 57 items: (a) 8 small seams, (b) 4 creation seams, (c) 45).
Target: SDK 1.0 == 0.7.0. Standing rulings honoured: host half stays in core · plugins are blind to connections (R53) ·
genres expect, never lock · approval binds to bundle bytes · sampling is config-referenced · no magic in core for pipeline
data (explicit steps) · SDK feels like Drizzle (typed, one way) · manifest limits validated at runtime.

## 0. One safety model for every new seam
1. **Declared** in the manifest, so the packager prints it and the admin reads it before enabling (same list as today's
   network/storage/events). Undeclared use is refused at install, never at run time.
2. **Granted** through the existing approval, bound to the bundle bytes: a changed bundle lands disabled, grants carried.
3. **Scoped by ownership.** Everything a plugin creates carries `owner: plugin:<name>`. A plugin reads/updates/deletes only
   what it owns; the person can edit or delete anything; the person's edits win over a plugin's later update (three-way
   merge, as `upgradeWorld` already does for worlds).
4. **Core does the writing.** Sandbox code never inserts rows: it hands core a document (a lorebook file, a card, a
   session request) and core's own importer / output step / start form does what a person's click would do, with the same
   validation, limits, write modes (Full / Review / Off) and audit.
5. **Quota'd** by the manifest limits block (`limits.create: { lorebooks, characters, entriesPerRun }`), defaults small.
6. **Audited and reversible.** Run-time creations are `pipeline_run_artifacts` rows (Back can retract them); install-time
   imports are listed on the plugin page; uninstall asks keep / delete for owned rows.
7. **Never silent.** Anything that opens a session, attaches a book or installs characters tells the person in the app
   (notify + the plugin page) — and a session is only ever created through the app's own start form.

## 1. Creation seams (b)
**b1 — books and cast at install (`provides`)** — closes "no characters to chat with".
- Manifest: `provides: { lorebooks: ['books/modern.lorebook.json', 'books/house.lorebook.json'] }` + `permissions.create.lorebooks`.
- On enable, core's own importer runs those files (the same path as Lorebooks › Import): entries, typed entries, the cards,
  avatars from package assets (copied into media with provenance `plugin`). Idempotent by `(plugin, book slug, version)`;
  an upgrade re-imports as a diff under the book's write mode (a Review book gets proposals). Owned rows are marked;
  uninstall asks keep / delete. Nothing runs in the sandbox.
- SDK: `defineExtension({ provides })` typed; `serene-pub check` validates the files with the importer's own validator.
- App: importer accepts any entry type a registered ruleset declares (**b4**), so routines/households import typed and
  core's cast-presences reads them.

**b1' — books and cast at run time (custom worlds)**
- New output steps, usable only in pipelines of the plugin's own genres: `core:output/create-lorebook@1`,
  `core:output/create-characters@1`, `core:output/attach-lorebook@1` (session-created only). Inputs are documents, not
  SQL-ish calls; outputs are artifact rows. Gated by `permissions.create.*` + `limits.create.*`; refused in a Review book
  → proposals. The session's annex/world state keeps living on the (now real) book.
- Portraits: a card's `avatar` may name a package asset (**a3**) or a storage file.

**b2 — a task can read storage.** `permissions.storage.read` for tasks (read-only, the plugin's own storage), plus
`core:query/plugin-storage@1`. A create pipeline can then load a custom world the person imported.

**b3 — a frame asks to start a session.** New frame request `{ t: 'create-session', genre, fields, lorebook? }` answered by
the APP opening its own start form prefilled; the person presses Start. Declared as `init.createSession` in the frame's
manifest block. No plugin ever creates a session unattended.

**Cast management after creation.** Owned characters are ordinary characters. A plugin may *propose* field updates to its
own characters on upgrade (same diff/merge as books); it may never touch characters it does not own. A `core:query/cast@1`
(already partly there) gives a pipeline the session cast with ownership marks.

## 2. Small seams (a) — pr-8 candidates, each one PR
- **a1** `file-save` hands the handler a writable sink (bytes or stream) and a suggested name; the frame gets a download.
- **a2** `file-pick` carries a `target` (which world / which field) so a handler can place a picked file.
- **a3** `ctx.package.read(path)` for handlers, read-only, allowlisted by the manifest `assets` block.
- **a4** frame CSP: `connect-src 'self'` for GET on `/plugin-assets/<pkg>/…` and `/plugin-data/<pkg>/…` only.
- **a5** a `json` field type: free-form object with a size cap (and an optional JSON schema the form validates).
- **a6** a halted run reaches the frame: the invoke response carries `{ halted: true, reason }`, plus a `run:ended`
  WidgetEvent `{ runId, outcome, reason? }`.
- **a7** keep every published ruleset version (a `ruleset_versions` table, or `published_versions` json); `vocabularyFor`
  resolves by the session's pin; conversion re-keys only values written under the old ids. Alternative: block or
  auto-convert pinned sessions at upgrade.
- **a8** `set-session-annex` takes `base` (compare-and-set), refusing a stale write with the fresh annex in the refusal.

## 3. Order and cost
1. pr-8: a6, a8, a4, a5 (small, each ≤ 1 lane); a1/a2/a3 together (handler I/O).
2. 0.7 / SDK 1.0: b1 + b4 (install-time import — biggest payoff, lowest risk, no sandbox code); then b2; then b3;
   then b1' (run-time creation) last, because it is the only seam where plugin pipelines write new rows.
3. a7 is a schema decision (★): rule "keep every version" vs "convert at upgrade" before any code.

## 4. What this does NOT add (by ruling)
No connection/model access for plugins (R53). No member-facing model plumbing. No plugin-created sessions. No
inline sampling. No plugin writes outside its ownership. No bypass of a book's write mode.

## 5. Owner findings while testing (2026-10-30 evening)
**Player identity = the persona.** The start form asked for "Your character" (name, bio) AND the app attached a persona.
Ruled: the persona is the player; the port keeps only what a persona cannot say (age, background) and reads name/bio from
the attached persona, falling back to a single Name when none is attached. Port change (S, in flight); no SDK seam.

**Mundi wants to manage sessions (saves).** In SillyTavern a save IS a chat, so Mundi's own New / Load / Delete-save screens
manage chats. In Serene Pub a save IS a session, and sessions belong to the app. Reconcile by widening **b3** from one
request to a small, person-confirmed navigation family a frame may send:
- `{ t: 'create-session', genre, fields, lorebook? }` → the app opens its own start form prefilled; the person presses Start.
- `{ t: 'open-sessions', genre }` → the app opens the Sessions list filtered to the plugin's genres (Mundi's "Load").
- `{ t: 'open-session', sessionId }` → only for a session the person can already open (Mundi's save list rows, read
  through the existing sessions grant).
- Delete / rename stay the app's: the frame may `open-session` and the person uses the app's own controls.
Declared as `init.sessions: ['create', 'open']` in the frame manifest block; the admin sees it before enabling; nothing
happens without a press in the app's own UI. Until this exists, the port's New game ends pointing to the app (readiness b3)
and "Load" is the Sessions dock.

## 6. "Apps" under Sessions (owner question 2026-10-30; recommended, not go)
A genre may declare `presentation: 'app'`. The Sessions rail shows an **Apps** group, one row per such genre; opening it
mounts the plugin's frame as a LAUNCHER without a session, full screen. The launcher reads the genre's sessions through the
existing sessions grant and uses §5's confirmed request family (create-session → the app's start form prefilled;
open-session; open-sessions). Each save stays an ordinary session (Back/Forward, history, panels, members unchanged) and
still appears in the ordinary list grouped under the app. NOT a singleton session: a home session would need a clock, book,
cast and history it has no use for and would collide with next chapter, deletion and the settle line. Fits the rulings:
genres expect never lock (a listing/entry flag only); host half stays in core (the launcher creates nothing); widget
functionality universal (a frame role any genre may declare, core ones included). Cost: one rail group, one session-less
frame mount, the §5 requests, one genre flag. Independent of §1; could land in 0.7 before or after it.
Entry-point rule (owner Q, 10-30): for an `app` genre, Sessions › New opens the LAUNCHER, whose New game screen is the start
form for the game's own fields (world, mode, difficulty, age, background, avatars) and sends create-session with them as the
genre's declared fields. The app then shows ITS start form with only what a plugin may not set (persona, lorebook, name
when no persona) — a one-press confirm when nothing is left. The generic start form stays reachable (genres expect, never
lock). One Apps row per plugin frame (Mundi offers World/Home inside); the genres stay separate session types underneath.
Continue = open-session on the newest; Load = the plugin's save list over the sessions grant, each row an open-session.

## 7. Per-user app data (owner Q 10-30)
Today a plugin has three homes and none is per user: (1) plugin storage — ONE root per plugin, pub-wide, one quota
(`plugins/<id>/…`, storageHost.ts), (2) per-session — the lorebook annex, session state/attributes, (3) per-viewer remount
memory — frame save-state (not storage). Proposed fourth home: **per-user plugin storage**. Manifest `permissions.storage.perUser`
(+ its own quota in `limits`); root `plugins/<id>/users/<uid>/` beside the app's own `data/users/<uid>/`; reached through the
SAME storage API with a `scope: 'user'` argument, resolved by core to the acting person (the presser of the action / the
viewer of the frame — never a user id the plugin names); no cross-user reads, no listing of users; removed with the account
and offered keep/delete on uninstall; served to the frame at `/plugin-data/<id>/me/<path>` only for the signed-in person.
Use: launcher preferences, per-person profile across saves, per-person import drafts. NOT for anything a save owns (that
stays in the session's annex) and NOT shared state (that is pub-wide storage).
Owner refinement (10-30): three homes, each × two scopes. FILES: pub-wide (exists) · per-user (§7). DATA (arbitrary
JSON, not files): a **plugin document store** — table `plugin_documents (plugin_id, scope: pub|user, owner_uid?, key,
doc jsonb, version, updated_at)`; per-session arbitrary data already = the lorebook annex, so no third scope there. API,
Drizzle-feeling and typed: `ctx.data(scope).get(key)` / `.set(key, doc, { base })` / `.list(prefix)` / `.remove(key)`,
available to handlers, tasks and frames (frame via request/response, same grant). `set` is compare-and-set on `version`
(the a8 lesson: no lost updates), refused with the fresh document. Caps in `limits.data` (docs per scope, bytes per doc,
total bytes); declared as `permissions.data: ['pub' | 'user']`; user scope resolved by core to the acting person, never
named by the plugin; removed with the account / keep-delete on uninstall; exported with the pub's backups. Not a query
engine: key + prefix only; anything relational belongs on the session or in a lorebook.
Naming (owner Q 10-30, proposed): NOT "app" (a presentation flag, not an owner — R1). The concept is the existing **annex**
(owner-namespaced JSON on a core entity, with data audiences), qualified by host (R3): session annex · lorebook annex ·
**user annex** · **pub annex**. Tables: `user_annexes (user_id, owner, key, doc, version, updated_at)` and
`pub_annexes (owner, key, doc, version, updated_at)` — rows, not one jsonb column, because per-key compare-and-set on
`version` is the point; `owner` = plugin id or `core`. Declaration = the existing annex field with `host: 'user' | 'pub'`.
API `annex('user').get/set/list/remove`. Files stay "plugin storage" / "per-user plugin storage". §24 check + §25 row when built.

## 8. Guide manages the library too — where the permissions split (owner 10-30)
Two actors want the same operations: a PLUGIN (third-party code; admin-approved; owns rows) and the GUIDE
(`core:genre/guide`, Serene: a model acting for a person inside that person's own rights). One operation surface,
two authority paths, one audit.
- **One surface — library operations in core:** `core:output/create-character@1` · `update-character@1` ·
  `create-lorebook@1` · `create-lore-entry@1` (exists) · `attach-lorebook@1` · `create-session` (request → the start form) ·
  the matching `core:query/*` reads. Documents in, artifact rows out (`pipeline_run_artifacts`): Back retracts, Session data
  shows, history explains. Nothing writes rows except these steps and the person's own clicks.
- **Authority path A — plugin:** manifest `permissions.create` + `limits`, admin grant bound to the bundle, OWNERSHIP scope
  (only its own rows, person's edits win). Unchanged from §1.
- **Authority path B — Guide (any core genre whose pipeline calls the model with these steps):** the acting PERSON's rights
  (what they could do by clicking: their characters, books they may edit, sessions they may open) and CONSENT, expressed with
  the dial that already exists for lorebooks — write modes **Full / Review / Off**, extended from "this book" to a per-person
  **library write mode** for Guide sessions (default Review: Serene proposes a card or a book, the person accepts in the
  session; Full: it lands and is undoable by Back; Off: Serene only drafts text). The genre declares the capability in
  `writes` (`writes.library: { characters, lorebooks, sessions }`, like `writes.lore/scenes`); the person's mode decides.
  Never more than the person has: a member cannot make Serene touch pipelines, loadouts, connections or other people's rows.
- **Where they meet:** a plugin genre whose pipeline uses the model to write (Mundi's diary) is already path B under the
  book's write mode; a plugin creating its own books at install is path A. A plugin may NOT borrow path B to write outside
  its ownership: path B only runs in a genre the person chose and under the person's mode, and the rows it makes are owned
  by the person, not the plugin.
- **Audit and undo are shared:** both paths produce the same artifact rows; the inspector shows "made by Serene for <person>"
  vs "made by plugin <name>".
- Order: build the surface once for §1 (plugins); Guide's path is the consent dial + `writes.library` on top, a small lane.
Owner ruling (10-30): a plugin's authority is the INTERSECTION of three things — its declared+granted permissions, its
ownership scope, and the acting person's own rights. A step runs as the person whose press, send or routine-owning
session caused it (a routine runs as the session owner); it can do nothing that person could not do by clicking. Install-time
work (provided books) runs as the admin who enabled the plugin. A guest's press cannot make a plugin write to the owner's
rows; a member's press cannot reach admin-only surfaces however the manifest reads. So path A = grant ∩ ownership ∩
person; path B = person ∩ consent mode. The "person" term is shared code: one resolver of what the acting person may do,
used by both paths and by the app's own UI (no second rights table).
Owner clarification (10-30): the split to design is **session permissions vs app permissions** (two declared groups, by
scope), not plugin-vs-Guide. **Session permissions** — what code may do inside the session it is mounted in or running
for: messages/channels, the session annex, state and attributes, actions/presses, invoke/response, lore writes under the
book's mode (`writes.lore/scenes`), file-pick/save, notify. **App permissions** — what code may do with the person's library
and the pub, outside any one session: characters, lorebooks (create/import/attach), sessions (list/open/create via the
start form), user annex and pub annex, plugin storage (pub-wide/per-user), package assets. Each group is declared, shown to
the admin and granted separately, so a frame that only plays a game asks for no app permission and a launcher asks for no
session permission. Both groups are bounded by the acting person (above). The Guide is a session that ALSO holds app
permissions (`writes.library`) under the person's consent mode — the same app group a plugin launcher would declare, so one
vocabulary serves plugins and core genres. §1's creation seams are the app group's write half; §5's session requests are
app permissions a session-mounted frame may additionally hold.
Owner Qs (10-30): **Pub annex — dropped from the first cut.** No port case needs it: plugin-wide settings are manifest fields
an admin sets; shared caches are plugin storage (files); anything per person is the user annex; anything per save is the
session annex. If a real case appears (a shared leaderboard, a cross-person cache) it is the same table with `owner` and no
user, added then. **Sessions — the app group gains edit and delete**, scoped to sessions of the plugin's own genres that the
acting person owns: `sessions.update` (title, cover, the genre's declared fields — Mundi's rename-a-save, difficulty) writes
directly under the app permission; `sessions.delete` is destructive, so the frame sends a request and the APP shows its own
confirm dialog (same pattern as create-session → start form). Never other genres' sessions, never another person's.
Owner (10-30): the sessions app permission is **genre-scoped by default and by declaration** — `sessions: { genres: ['mundi:genre/lifesim', 'mundi:genre/scenario'] }`,
each id one the package itself declares (a package may not name another plugin's or core's genre; the packager refuses it).
Every sessions operation (list, open, create, update, delete, and the launcher's save list read) is filtered to those genres
∩ the acting person's own sessions. There is no "all genres" value for a plugin; a core genre such as the Guide declares
its scope the same way, or `self`.

## 9. Three front-end APIs (owner 10-30)
**ui** — host chrome, no data: notify, fullscreen, file-pick/save, translate, save-state (remount memory), theme/locale/viewer,
and the confirmed navigation requests (open the start form, open a session, open the Sessions list, delete-confirm).
**session** — the mounted session: messages/channels, session annex, state/attributes, actions/presses, invoke/response,
lore grant + lorebook annex, session-scoped queries. **applet** — the person's library and the pub outside any one session:
characters, lorebooks (create/import/attach), sessions (list/open/create/update/delete, genre-scoped), user annex, plugin
storage (pub/per-user), package assets.
Who gets what: a **widget** (placed in the normal session layout) declares ui + session only — the packager refuses applet on
a widget. An **applet frame** (the launcher role) declares ui + applet, and holds session as well only while mounted in a
session (Mundi: the same frame is launcher without a session, game with one). Every grant is still ∩ the acting person.
Naming (R1/R2/R4): NOT "app" for the plugin-provided thing — "the app" already means Serene Pub itself throughout canon,
docs and this plan. **applet** is distinctive and unclaimed: the role (`presentation: 'applet'`), the API (`applet`), the
permission group (`permissions.applet`). The rail label may still read **Apps** (§24 rule 6: a UI label is not a type name).
§24 check + §25 rows when built.

## 10. A core applet (owner Q 10-30; recommended)
Ship ONE: the **Dungeon Crawler** as the first core applet — launcher with a campaigns list, Continue (newest), New campaign
(the interview, start form reduced to its confirm), rename/difficulty via sessions.update, delete via the confirm. Its
session is unchanged. Reason: a seam core never uses rots (the showcase-plugins rule); the Crawler needs every applet
permission a core genre would. Conditions: built on the SDK surface only (no private door); AFTER the Mundi port has walked
the applet API (first customer the port, second core). Chat/Story stay conversations; the Lair keeps its in-session shell;
the Guide stays a session with library permissions under the consent mode. 0.7, behind the creation seams.

## 12. Re-read against CLAUDE.md of 2026-11-04 (Principles P1–P6, Decisions, Guides) — audit of what M2 built
Each built thing tested against the owner's principles. ✓ = holds; ★ = propose-and-wait item for the owner (one recommendation + trade-off, below in §14).
- **P1 Deterministic, never hidden.**
  - Library writes by the Guide: genre opt-in (`writes.library`), every write a proposal/ledger line with its cause and Back to undo ✓. Off writes a "not added" line naming the setting ✓.
  - Provided books import on ENABLE (an admin's press), listed on the plugin page with state and reason ✓; the tombstone makes a hand delete stick ✓.
  - Busy door, run:ended, annex compare-and-set: code decides, the model only judges ✓.
  - ★A The Guide's hidden `<!--draft:…-->` block is an automatic behaviour inside the reply: it is genre-opted and visible as a proposal, but the *extraction* step runs on every Guide turn. Holds P1 (deterministic, cause shown); flag only that it is a new default for the Guide genre, which the owner may want off until asked.
- **P2 Modular.** User annex, per-user storage, provided books, ruleset versions each have one owner module and one write path ✓. ★B The acting-person resolver sits *beside* the app's per-handler checks (two deciders of one question, by the 10-31 ruling "converge later") — a P2 debt with a date, not a bug; convergence is a named stage below.
- **P3 Few settings, no admin for everyday use.** New settings introduced by M2: (1) **library write mode** (per person; who: the person; why: consent before a model writes their library; without it: either silent writes or no Guide library help) ✓ passes the test, default Review needs no action; (2) **permission groups Allow/Deny all** (admin, install-time only) ✓; (3) **uninstall keep/delete checkboxes** (admin, rare) ✓; (4) **per-user quota on the declaration** — author-side, not a user setting ✓. No everyday setting was added. ★C A plugin's preset arriving "not available" forces an admin step before a member can play (fails P3's "plays after one guided step" for plugin genres) — recommendation stands: enabling the plugin makes its presets available.
- **P4 Power controls purposeful.** The three permission groups each carry one sentence ✓. ★D The Leave pill and Apps row have no purpose sentence in the UI (none needed for a navigation control; noted only).
- **P5 Derived from the SDK.** The launcher's prefilled start form and its one-press confirm are derived from the genre's declared fields ✓; the permission review is derived from compiled keys ✓; the `json` field renders from its declaration ✓. ★E Declarations without purpose/group/reach do not yet get the validation *warning* P5 asks for (the packager has E_* refusals, no "lands under Advanced" warning class) — a small CLI lane.
- **P6 Charters.** Every M2 name has a §25 row; the rows record the change, not a charter (purpose · why · owns · does not own · who does). ★F A charter pass over the M2 rows (applet, appletApi events, user annex, per-user storage, provided book, library write mode, ruleset version, acting person, FrontEndApi) — one NOMENCLATURE lane, no code.
- **Decisions rule "nothing for later".** M2 built no unused hooks: b1' (plugin run-time creation) is *refused*, not stubbed ✓; the four `applet` request kinds that had no server in M2b got servers in M2c ✓; `plugin-storage` is the only kind still answering "not available yet" from a frame — it is a query node for pipelines by design; ★G decide whether frames get it or the kind is removed.
- **sdk:build rule (new wording):** never while any serene-pub `vite dev` runs (`pgrep -af "vite dev"` + cwd check), else report "blocked on your dev server". M2 lanes checked :5173 only; the M3 briefs must carry the new rule verbatim.

## 13. Next stages, re-planned under the 11-04 guides (`.claude/guides/multi-agent.md`, `review.md`) — NOT go
- Before any lane: a **plan-challenger** reads this plan cold (review.md §1). After each lane: a **scope-reviewer** on the diff against its brief (§2), then `/code-review`.
- Shape: ≤ 2 `lane-supervisor` at once, ≤ 2 `lane-worker` each; fresh agents per stage (never resume a long one); briefs at `plans/briefs/BRIEF-<lane-id>.md`, reports at `plans/REPORT-<lane-id>-<date>.md` (repo-relative `plans/`; this plan and the M2 briefs live under `~/.claude/plans/` — ★H move or leave, owner's call); every implementation brief carries the four verbatim lines from multi-agent.md; `archex` first for "where is X".
- **M2d — convergence + charters (small, core only):** (a) ★B resolver convergence: the app's per-handler checks call `personMay`, one decider; (b) ★F charter pass on the M2 NOMENCLATURE rows; (c) ★E the P5 validation warning class in the packager; (d) ★48 create-session's undeclared field checked at the request. Lanes: S (resolver), T (NOMENCLATURE), P (packager), U (request). One E gate.
- **M3 — the port's 0.2 (≈ 0.5 × M1):** launcher screens (New / Load / Continue / rename / delete) over the applet API; `provides.lorebooks` for the two shipped worlds (drops the manual derived-book import); user annex for per-person profile; `update-session`/`delete-session`; the frame reads `halted`/`run:ended` (★9); `applet: true` on both genres; README/CHANGELOG; the port's own gate walk. Lanes U (frame) · S (genre/presets/pipelines) · P (manifest/provides/package) · T (engine touch-ups). Port repo only; "Mundi" allowed there.
- **M4 — Dungeon Crawler as the first core applet (§10):** after M3 has walked the applet API once. Lanes U + S.
- Not planned (by ruling): plugin run-time creation steps (b1'); pub annex; a tool loop in the Guide.

## 14. Open ★ for the owner — propose-and-wait (one recommendation + its trade-off each)
From E's consolidated list (briefs/m2c-E-REPORT.md, 50 items, 34 ruled) plus §12's audit:
- ★7 `ctx.package.read` paths are package-relative → **keep** (matches `assetsBase`'s tail); trade-off: authors type `assets/…` twice.
- ★9 a frame shows nothing on a halted press unless it reads `halted`/`run:ended` → **M3 port work**, no core change.
- ★11 uninstall keep/delete as a plugin-page checkbox; bulk keeps → **keep** (rare admin act); trade-off: no per-item choice in bulk.
- ★18 user-annex caps as core constants vs P's declaration quota → **unify on the declaration in M2d** (one place authors read); trade-off: a hostile cap needs the ceiling clamp P already uses.
- ★19 a commit conflict leaves storage files written → **document as a readiness item**; a transactional file store is out of scope.
- ★20 SDK api pages not regenerated by docs:build → **add to docs:build** (one script line).
- ★27 a Review/Off provided book holds an upgrade → **keep holding** until book-level proposals exist (needs a table; not now).
- ★34 no Restore for a dismissed provided book → **add a Restore on the plugin page in M2d** (one button; the tombstone already holds what it needs); trade-off: one more admin control (P4 sentence: "Bring back a book you dismissed").
- ★39 a plugin's preset arrives not available → **enabling the plugin makes its presets available** (P3); trade-off: an admin who wanted to hide one preset hides it after.
- ★43 Back on a made lorebook deletes, Forward re-imports under a new id → **accept until lorebook soft delete** (its own plan).
- ★46 the Guide's "See: /docs/…" line → **render as a link inside the reply, or drop when no excerpt was used** (cosmetic, T).
- ★47 default Reply sampling 512 tokens + reasoning unset → **seed a reasoning-off dialogue config at 1024+ tokens and make it the Reply default on a fresh install** (P3: plays after one step); trade-off: longer replies on small models.
- ★48 create-session's undeclared field refused only at Start → **check at the request** (M2d, U).
- ★49 should a plugin genre ever declare `writes.library`? → **no, until the applet permission for it exists as a declared, admin-granted key** (then path A rules apply); today refused.
- ★50 a package-ruleset entry counted in Everything but listed in no view → **list it under its ruleset's lens even when the book does not play that ruleset** (small, lorebooks UI).
- ★A/★C/★E/★F/★G/★H from §12 as written there.

## 16. Owner changes 2026-11-04 (GO given the same day: "go ahead and start implementing the plan")
Three changes to the applet surface, taken as the plan's intent over §6/§13 where they differ. Names below are 🚧 (§24 grep by the lane
that builds them; ★N2 lists them for rename). "app" stays Serene Pub; **applet** stays the ruled word; the UI label stays **Apps**.

**C1 — Apps is its own view, under Sessions.** A rail view `apps` (title _Apps_, `LayoutGrid`) in the Play group right after Sessions
(`CONTENT_ORDER` AND `rightNavOrder` — the mobile More sheet reads the second — sessions · apps · characters …; address `/apps` in `VIEW_ROUTES`; a cold load of `/apps` focuses the view over Home like
`/sessions`). Its list column is a **card grid** at the `reading` fill width (§5.2 rule for card grids): one **applet card** per plugin,
**16:9**, carrying the applet's **poster** (the picture, `object-cover`), its **logo** (optional, a small square over the poster's lower-left),
its title (15px medium) and a **blurb** (one short line, the meta line's lead, clamped to two lines) with its genre names as the facts. A press
opens the launcher `/apps/<pluginId>` as today. No poster → a `surface-200-800` ground with the `LayoutGrid` glyph, so a bare applet still
lists. The Sessions view's **Apps** group (M2c, `appletRows` in `SessionsSidebar`) goes: one home for the list (R1); the entry-point rule
(Sessions › New › an applet genre opens its launcher; _Use the start form_) stays. The view registers only while at least one applet is
listed (★N1), the same rule as C2, so a fresh install shows no empty rail item (P3); a cold `/apps` with no applet listed goes Home (never a blank page).

**C2 — Home lists applets.** When at least one applet is listed, Home shows an **Apps** section with the same cards, after _Pick up where you
left off_ and before _Characters_ (★N3 position). Absent otherwise; never an empty state on Home.

**C3 — The card's art is declared by the package.** `ExtensionDecl.appletCard?: { poster?: string; logo?: string; blurb?: I18n }` (qualified per R3: `shape.applet` is the genre flag, `permissions.applet` the grant group) — paths
package-relative under the declared `assets.dir` (as `provides.lorebooks`, ★7 ruled package-relative), served from the existing
`/plugin-assets/<id>/<version>/<path>`; `blurb` at most 160 characters. Packager: `E_APPLET_ART_OUTSIDE_ASSETS` · `E_APPLET_ART_MISSING` ·
`E_APPLET_BLURB_LONG`, repeated at install (codes may read `E_APPLET_CARD_*`, lane D's §24 call) (manifest limits validated at runtime). No aspect-ratio check: the card crops; docs say 16:9,
1280×720 recommended. A declaration without an applet genre is `E_APPLET_ART_WITHOUT_APPLET`. Wire: `appletMount` returns
`{ src, title, poster?, logo?, blurb?, genres, grants }` (URLs, resolved text); the `sessions:genres` row's `appletTitle` becomes
**`appletCard?: { title, poster?, logo?, blurb? }`** (not `applet`: `shape.applet` already sits on the row) (present exactly when the launcher mounts — the M2c rule, one word); `appletRows` reads it.
One source for the Apps view, Home and the launcher.

**C4 — Launching loads like a game.** The launcher shows an **applet loading screen** over the frame from mount until the frame's `ready`
(protocol 2, already sent): the poster as a dimmed backdrop (ground `surface-100-900` without one), the logo or title centred, an
indeterminate `SpProgress` and _Loading <title>…_; it fades over `MOTION.slow` (a still under reduced motion). A `fatal` frame error ends it
with the frame's sentence; after 20 s without `ready` it reads _Still loading…_ — **Leave** and Esc work throughout. Deterministic: code
ends it on `ready`, never on a timer alone (P1). The same screen covers a session-mounted applet frame (`session-view` of an applet genre)
until its `ready`; a run already in flight on that session is the session page's affair (ember, the log) — the frame reads `halted`/`run:ended`
itself (★9, M3). No art, no sound, no minimum duration.

**C5 — Rule: applet-API code runs only while its applet is open; session-API code runs in the background like any session's.**
**HELD for the owner (challenger objection 2, 2026-11-04): the sentence has two readings and nothing is built until one is ruled.**
The owner's words: "Applet code/pipelines can only run when the applet is open. While session api pipelines can run in the background?"
- ★N4 (a) ★ **recommended** — *by cause*: applet-group reach (user annex, per-user storage, the library doors) is granted to a call only when
  a mounted frame's own request or the person's press in it caused the call. That covers every hook kind — event, lifecycle, the schedule tick
  AND pipeline nodes (query · outlet · oracle) — whenever a routine, cadence or schedule caused the run; a press-caused turn keeps it. Session
  reach (session annex, lore under the book's mode, turns, cadences) runs with nobody looking. Trade-off: an applet cannot tidy a person's
  profile between visits; a new "cause" field rides on the call (`HookCtxGrants` gains a member, a published type).
- ★N4 (b) — *by genre*: an applet genre's pipelines as a whole run only while its frame is mounted (a turn in flight when the person leaves
  is halted, the halt recorded with its cause). Trade-off: a Mundi save's turn stops when the tab closes; session semantics differ by genre.
Until ruled, the applet doors stay as built (a frame-only reach by construction) and the hook grant table is untouched.

**C6 — No Resume overlay, no pause state** (owner Q 11-04, recommended, not objected): a run finishes or halts, nothing suspends; the
loading screen (C4) and the launcher's Continue (M3) are the return path. Listed under not planned.

★N1 the Apps view registers only while an applet is listed (else an empty rail item on every fresh install) — trade-off: the rail changes
when a plugin is enabled. ★N2 names: `appletCard` (the declaration block and the row), **poster**, **logo**, **blurb** (blurb = the 12px second line's word in
§5.2 already), **applet card**, **applet loading screen**. ★N3 Home position. ★N4 above.

after each lane; the lead runs one grouped verification. Briefs `plans/briefs/BRIEF-m2d-<lane>.md`, reports `plans/REPORT-m2d-<lane>-<date>.md`
(repo-relative per the guides; ★H: this plan stays under `~/.claude/plans/`).
- **Lane D — SDK + server** (`m2d-D`; owns `../serene-pub-sdk/{sdk,cli}/src`, the SDK's `guides/` and `scripts/` (the generated permissions guide), `src/lib/server/**`, `src/lib/shared/**`, `scripts/build-docs.js`; migrations none):
  D1 SDK: C3 declaration + packager refusals; ★E the P5 validation **warning** class
  (purpose/group/reach missing on a user-facing declaration → warning, "lands under Advanced"); ★18 user-annex caps read from the declaration
  (core ceiling clamp kept); ★20 docs:build regenerates the SDK api pages. D2 server: ★B resolver convergence — the app's per-handler checks
  call `personMay`, one decider; C3 `appletMount` art + `sessions:genres` `applet` row; ★46 the Guide's _See: /docs/…_ line rendered as a link or dropped when no
  excerpt was used.
- **Lane C — client + docs** (`m2d-C`; owns `src/lib/client/**`, `src/routes/**`, `NOMENCLATURE.md`, `STYLE-GUIDE.md`, `docs/**`):
  C1 Apps view + applet card + the Sessions group's removal; C2 Home section; C4 loading screen; ★48 create-session's undeclared field checked
  at the request. T (docs, one worker): ★F charter pass on the M2 rows + the §16 rows + §25 ledger; STYLE-GUIDE §5.1/§6.4/§6.7;
  `docs/sessions.md` Apps → `docs/getting-around.md` + `docs/plugins.md` (art; the C5 sentence only once ruled); CHANGELOG line.
- Wire contract fixed by §16 so the lanes build in parallel: the row shape, the mount shape, the declaration shape, the grant table.
- **Left open, not built (challenger round 1, 11-04):** ★39 and ★47 (change a fresh install); ★N4 (C5, two readings); **★34** Restore (a new
  admin control and a new socket event — a user sees it) and **★50** (changes what a lorebook view lists) — both wait for a yes. Built because
  they change nothing a user sees or were ruled: ★B, ★E (warning only), ★F, ★18, ★20, ★46, ★48, C1–C4, C6.
  ★27 keep holding · ★43 accept · ★19 a readiness line · ★49 no · ★11 keep · ★7 keep · ★9 M3.
- Then M3 (port 0.2) and M4 (Crawler applet) as §13, on budget.
- **Challenger round 1 (2026-11-04, one round):** (1) open ★ built on a general go — *accept in part*: ★34/★50 held (user-visible), the rest built as
  invisible or ruled; the C3 SDK names are built 🚧 under ★N2 (pre-releases are disposable). (2) C5 reads two ways — *needs owner* (★N4 a/b
  above; lane D builds nothing of it). (3) `applet` thrice — *accept*: `appletCard`. (4) orphan files / contract gaps — *accept*: SDK guides+scripts
  and `build-docs.js` to lane D; the per-user-storage grant column and the Restore event fall with C5/★34. (5) both order arrays, `/apps` goes Home
  — *accept*.

## 19. Owner rulings 2026-11-04 (evening) and M2e — GO ("Yes, M2e now")
Rulings, each quoted from the owner's answers:
- **C5 → by cause (★N4 a).** Applet-group reach (user annex, per-user storage, the library doors) is granted only to a call a mounted frame's own request or a person's press in it caused; event/lifecycle hooks, the schedule tick and pipeline nodes on routine/cadence/schedule runs get none; session reach runs in the background.
- **★39 → presets available on enable.** Enabling a plugin makes its session presets available; an admin hides one afterwards if wanted.
- **★47 → seed it.** A fresh install gets a reasoning-off dialogue sampling config at 1024+ tokens, the Reply default.
- **★46 → keep in the task**; one contract-doc line on `core:task/read-library-draft@1`.
- **★34 → "immutable lorebook starters (from core, plugin, etc.) can only be disabled, not deleted"**, disabled = **hidden everywhere, kept intact**: leaves the Lorebooks list, pickers and attach menus; rows stay; the plugin page (and the admin Starter lorebooks page for core's) lists it with an Enable switch; Delete is removed from provided/starter books; the tombstone becomes the disabled flag; upgrades still land while disabled.
- **★50 → list under its ruleset's lens.** · **★B soft-deleted → accept.** · **★C-1 Leave → back where you came from** (Apps when entered from Apps, Home from Home, Sessions as the fallback). · **★C-2 → keep `LayoutGrid`.** · **★18 → keep the constants.** · **★E-2 → yes**: a setting with no group lands under an Advanced fold in every derived form. · **★H → the plan moves into the repo** (done; `~/.claude/plans/` copy is a pointer). · **★C-3 → accept** (host's sentence on fatal).
Lanes (same shape as §17; briefs `plans/briefs/BRIEF-m2e-<lane>.md`, reports `plans/REPORT-m2e-<lane>-2026-11-04.md`):
- **Lane D — SDK + server (`m2e-D`):** C5 by cause (the grant derivation reads the acting person's cause — `actingPersonFor`'s `via` already says `press` vs `routine`; a frame's door is a press by construction — and refuses with one sentence elsewhere; hook grant table + app `hookCtx` + guide regen); ★39 presets available on enable; ★47 the seeded Reply sampling config (boot pass + a second-boot-changes-nothing test; no hardcoded id); ★34 server half (provided/starter books: `disabled` flag replaces the tombstone; `lorebooks:delete` refuses a starter with the sentence; enable/disable admin event; pickers/attach/list queries exclude disabled; `syncProvidedBooks` upgrades disabled books too; the data on disk: a migration or boot pass only if a column is needed — the tombstone json may carry `disabled`); ★46 contract line in `contracts/src/tasks.ts`; the queued shared `declaredFieldRefusal` sentence in `src/lib/shared`.
- **Lane C — client + docs (`m2e-C`):** ★34 client half (Delete gone from starter/provided book rows and detail; Disable/Enable on the plugin page and the admin Starter lorebooks page with one purpose sentence each; disabled books hidden from the list, pickers and attach menus); ★50 lens listing; ★C-1 Leave back where you came from; ★E-2 the Advanced fold in the form deriver; NOMENCLATURE §25 rows (disabled starter, the cause rule in the applet charter, Advanced fold), STYLE-GUIDE (§6.11 admin, §6.4 rows), docs (`plugins.md` C5 sentence + disabled books, `lorebooks.md` starters, `getting-around.md` Leave).
- Not in M2e: ★N1–N3 stand as built; no pause state; no plugin run-time creation.
- **Challenger round 1 on §19 (2026-11-04, one round) — all five accepted; §19 reads with these corrections:**
  1. **C5 by cause is NOT `via`.** `press` is inferred from any `scope.userId` (host.ts ~2389, write.ts ~1297), the applet door resolves as `view` (appletDoor.ts:55), and plugin calls carry only `user` (eventHost.ts:621, pluginBindings.ts:381, sessionEvents.ts:211). Build a **frame-origin cause**: set only by the applet door (`sockets/appletCause.ts` `markAppletCall` already marks a frame's call) and by runs a frame's request starts (the `fromApplet` create/press paths); carried on the run scope and on each sandbox call beside `user`; the applet-group grant (user annex, per-user storage) derives from it. The addition to the published call/ctx types is the trade-off the owner accepted with "By cause".
  2. **★34 covers three cases**, not one: (a) **provided books** — the flag lives in `plugins.provided`; (b) **core and plugin primers** (`starterKits.ts:7–10`: catalogue data, no row) — "disabled" = hidden from _Start from a starter lorebook_; their switch needs a storage home → **★M2e-1** to the owner (see the question asked 11-04); (c) **admin kits** (`lorebooks` rows of kind `starter_kit`) — NOT immutable (ruling 2026-10-21 (5): clone and edit), so **Delete starter lorebook stays** and nothing changes. A session already reading a disabled book keeps reading it; **only the server hides** (one decider): list/picker/attach readers exclude it, the queries that read books into a session do not; lane C draws what it is sent.
  3. **★E-2's trigger is exactly the warning's** (missing description, group or reach on a user-facing field), one SDK-exported predicate shared by `cli/src/compiler.ts:1773` and the server deriver `src/lib/server/sessions/settingsForm.ts:277,667` — so the fold is **lane D's**; lane C only draws it. Lane D reports which core fields would move; where an everyday field would (core genres declare `group` once, `core-catalog/src/registry/genres.ts:206`), fix the declaration in core-catalog, never the rule.
  4. **Tombstone → disabled is a stored-data change already shipped** (0140 is in v0.6.0-pr-7): a **boot pass** turns each `dismissed[slug]` into `disabled[slug]` and the next sync re-imports the book as disabled; a test that a second boot changes nothing.
  5. **Lane D's files gain `../serene-pub-sdk/core-catalog/src` and `contracts/src`**; ★47 is one seed literal in `defaults.ts` (a `seedKey`, no `id`, `isImmutable`, `responseTokens` ≥ 1024, reasoning resolving to off) plus the Reply loadout's `sampling` key in `core-catalog/src/loadouts.ts:44` (resolved on insert only = fresh installs only, `loadouts/seed.ts:9–11`); the new row joins `defaults.seedKey.int.test.ts`; no new pass. ★39 is one line: `registrySync.ts:977` `enabled: decl.enabled === true` on insert.
- **★M2e-1 ruled (owner, 11-04): "Primers too, as rows."** Every core and plugin primer (today catalogue data behind _Start from a starter lorebook_, `starterKits.ts`) is imported at boot as an **immutable lorebook row**, the way a provided book is — owner `core` for core's (the stored `core` plugin row exists), the plugin for a plugin's — idempotent by (owner, slug, version), upgraded as a diff, hidden with the same Disable/Enable as a provided book; the _Start from a starter lorebook_ picker reads those rows (a person's start is still a clone; the clone is theirs and deletable; admin starter kits unchanged). One mechanism, three sources. Existing installs gain the rows on the next boot (a boot pass through the provided-book sync; clones already made are separate rows and untouched); a second boot changes nothing. Lane D builds the import + hiding, lane C the admin Starter lorebooks page (rows with the switch) and the picker.
- **Owner rulings 2026-11-04 (late):** ★Q-3 accept for 0.6 (the session-less screen's trust = the other applet doors'; the owner calls the Apps card "the launcher" — vocabulary note, no rename asked) · ★Q-4 keep narrow (press-door fires only) · ★M2e-D-1 keep (uninstall-with-keep un-hides) · ★M2e-D-2 rename the two field-group headings **Writing** (was Reply) and **Lair** (was Story) · ★M2e-D-3 accept (primer cards in the admin's Characters) · ★M2e-D-4 primer rows read-only, clone only · ★Q-2 keep `fromPressDoor` · §23 retire `dismissed` · switch copy accepted · ★D3-1 build now. Lanes **m2e-F-D** (headings + pins, primer read-only server, session-creation regression triage) and **m2e-F-C** (★D3-1, read-only client, NOMENCLATURE/docs, two svelte-check errors) launched.
- **Owner, late 11-04, on ★F-D-1 (a primer row attached directly to a session):** "Don't need to make the verbage so complicated. Use an existing lorebook, or use a primer/starter. Have it create a new lorebook that superimposes on the primer. On save as a real lorebook, then clone and merge." → **No attach refusal built.** Recorded as the next lorebook design item, 🚧 **overlay book** (name per §24 when planned): a session started from a primer gets a new lorebook that reads through to the primer and holds only the session's own writes; _Save as a lorebook_ clones the primer and merges the overlay into the clone. Needs its own plan (reads through two books, the attic/export shape, the settle line) — not M2e. ★F-D-2 (import overwrite): already refused — the only overwrite path is the resolve action, which checks `primerRowEditProblem`.
- **Item 0 root cause (F-D):** not a wrong handle — a static import added to `db/defaults.ts` by playtest2-C pulled `utils/sessionAccess.ts` onto the real database's boot path, so int tests' mocked db never reached it; fixed at the import graph (`utils/androidWrapper.ts` leaf). ## 22. PROPOSED (owner direction 2026-11-05, with ★M3-1) — the start form chooses a book, and the book brings the rules — not go
Owner: "merge ruleset selection into lorebook selection, so starting a new phantom lorebook is choosing a ruleset blank book or a primer to start. Lorebooks and rulesets should both show ruleset compatibility with the genre/preset." Reading: the start form's two choices (a lorebook, and Rules / How strict) become ONE: pick an existing lorebook (its rules come with it), a **blank book of a ruleset** (today's "no book" + a rules choice, as one row per ruleset the genre fits), or a **primer** (a provided/primer row, cloned — or, once it exists, the overlay book). Every row says whether its rules fit the genre/preset (the genre's `rulesets: { only | prefer }` and the preset's expectation — "genres expect, never lock": a misfit is a sentence, not a refusal). The Rulesets view and the Lorebooks view show the same fit words. ★ recommended shape: one lane on the start form (`StartSessionForm.svelte`, `startSessionRules`), one on the server (`sessions:create` takes the book choice and derives the ruleset; the phantom book is created with the chosen ruleset), one on the two views' fit words. Needs the overlay book first? No — a primer start clones today. Goes after M3; the owner says go.
- **Ruling (owner, 2026-11-05, with §22):** "Rulesets are stateless and carry just that, rules/scripts/prose that genres/sessions can act on." Written into the **ruleset** charter (NOMENCLATURE §24 rulesets vocabulary). For §22 it means: the book carries the state and names its rules; a "blank book of a ruleset" is a new phantom book playing that ruleset, and the ruleset itself never holds a book, a value or a session.
- **m3-core (2026-11-05):** built (`plans/REPORT-m3-core-2026-11-05.md`): provided books readable pub-wide (`providedBooksReadableBy`, the share projection, attach admitted), the applet genre's in-session frame holds `ui`+`session`+`applet` (`appletGrants` on `sessions:view`, `sessionViewMount`), `init.mount` (optional) on the SDK frame protocol. Lane's own scope round: ★M3-core-1 (HIGH) a member's attach minted cast rows straight into the provided book → **lead fixed**: `runLorebookBindingCheck` mints nothing when the session's owner does not own the book (+ test with a member seating a character); ★M3-core-2 primer rows stay "started from, never read into a session" — **accept** (the brief's sentence was wrong); ★M3-core-3 `mount?` optional — **accept**. Lead also: the start/edit forms' book pickers keep a provided row (`!isSharedWithMe || provided`), the launcher uses `appletFrontEndApis(false)`. Open copy ★: a member's provided row reads "Shared with you · From <admin>" in the Lorebooks view ("Comes with <plugin>" would be truer).
- **M3 closed 2026-11-05** (detail and ★ list in `~/.claude/plans/briefs/mundi-M3-PLAN.md` "M3 status"): the port's 0.2.0 packaged; the gate walked as admin and as a member on an isolated instance; the core side (`m3-core` + the lead's `init.inSession`, the binding-check ownership gate, picker filters, the disk install's enabling admin, the import's cause in the log) is in this tree. Core items Noticed, not done: a grant approval should run the provided-book sync (or say so); the importer should refuse one bad typed row, not the whole book; the overview counts halted presses as "failed runs". Owner ★ carried over: ★M3-3 (minimal seed without a persona), ★M3-4 (seating a book's cast from the launcher — ★ a genre-declared behaviour), the "Shared with you · From admin" copy for a provided row.
- **§22 GO (owner, 2026-11-05 evening: "Go after this round's fixes") — challenger round 1, five objections, all taken:**
  1. **HIGH, accepted — merge only where the genre declares `shape.lorebook`.** Dungeon Crawler has no lorebook step yet offers Rules and How strict (`core-catalog genres.ts` ~1393/1419; `StartSessionForm.svelte` `showRules` ~1005; `docs/sessions.md` 24–25). A genre without a lorebook step keeps today's Rules card. **How strict is a session setting, never the book's** (books hold only `primary_ruleset` + `ruleset_version`): it stays its own field after the book pick, under `rigourFor`/`rigourLocked` as today.
  2. **HIGH, accepted — the three rows are today's three request shapes; "phantom" is retired (§23 retired words, 2026-10-25): say *own book*.** An existing book (a provided book included — attached, never cloned: §23's seating needs the provided-marked cards of the attached book) sends `lorebookId`; a *blank book of a ruleset* sends no book + `primaryRuleset` and the session's **own book** is made on that ruleset (`sessions.ts` ~1296); a *starter* (primer row / admin kit) sends `starterKit` and is copied into the own book (`bookFromKit`, ~1306). The server lane shrinks to whatever gap the start-form lane finds; no "derive the ruleset" rewrite.
  3. **HIGH — ★§22-1 for the owner: a Freeform book.** "An existing book brings its rules" reverses **fix7** (2026-10-23): a book on Freeform (every new book's default, `BOOK_DEFAULT_RULES`) brings no rules of its own and the Rules picker stays (`shared/rulesets/rulesOffer.ts`, `sessions.ts` ~1142). Recommendation ★: a Freeform book's row keeps a rules choice under it ("‹book› with ‹ruleset›"); a book with a ruleset fixes it. Built as the recommendation unless ruled otherwise.
  4. **MEDIUM — ★§22-2 for the owner: `only` locks today.** The SDK spells the declaration `rulesets: { only } | { recommended, default }` (no `prefer`); `only` refuses a book/starter/pick outside the list (`sessions.ts` ~1153–1195, `genreOffer.ts` ~60) and both port genres declare it. "A misfit is a sentence, not a refusal" would change a published SDK name's meaning (the owner's call) or leave `only` as a ruled exception to "genres expect, never lock". Recommendation ★: keep `only` as the ruled exception (a genre whose rules *are* the game), write the plan with `recommended` for the fit words.
  5. **MEDIUM — ★§22-3 for the owner: the fit words need a calculation that does not exist outside the start form.** Fit is per genre (`genreRulesOffer` → `rulesetFit(genre, ruleset)`), only for genres declaring `uses` (Story, Lair, Dungeon Crawler); none per preset (a preset pins or locks); the Rulesets view is admin-only with no genre in context. Recommendation ★: a ruleset row shows fit per enabled genre that declares `uses` ("Story: Great · Lair: Good · Crawler: Poor"), a preset only "Set by preset ‹name›" where it locks; the Lorebooks view shows the book's ruleset's fit the same way. Lanes wait on ★§22-1…3; §22's lane shape is then: **(a)** the start form's one pick (merged where `lorebook` is declared; Freeform per ★§22-1; How strict kept), **(b)** the fit words (per ★§22-3) in the Rulesets and Lorebooks views, **(c)** server only for gaps.
- **Owner rulings 2026-11-05 (evening) on the three ★ — all three recommendations taken:** ★§22-1 a Freeform book's row keeps a rules choice under it ("‹book› with ‹ruleset›"); a book that names a ruleset fixes it; **fix7 stands**. ★§22-2 **`only` stays a lock, as the ruled exception** to "genres expect, never lock": a genre whose rules *are* the game may refuse other rules; no SDK rename; §22's fit words use `recommended`. ★§22-3 the fit words: a ruleset row shows fit per enabled genre that declares `uses` ("Story: Great · Lair: Good · Dungeon Crawler: Poor"); a book row the same for its ruleset; a preset only "Set by preset ‹name›" where it locks; today's `rulesetFit`, no new calculation. Lanes **s22-C** (start form), **s22-V** (the two views' fit words) and **s22-S** (server gaps, if any, named by C within its first hour) follow.

## 23. M3b — a launcher start seats the world's people and makes the player (owner rulings 2026-11-05, GO: "We need to do those")
Rulings: **★M3-4 → "The book seats its cast, genre-declared"**: a genre declares that attaching the lorebook seats its cast members as the session's characters, whoever owns the cards; reading follows the book's pub-wide access; off for every other genre (P1). **★M3-3 → "Make the persona from the wizard"**: the launcher's wizard creates the player's persona card from what it asked (name, age, looks, traits) through the app's own door on the person's press, and attaches it; a person who already has one may pick it instead.
Contract (lanes build against it; names 🚧 per §24, the lane's call):
- **SDK** `SessionShape.castFromLorebook?: { field?: string }` (🚧 name) — _seat the attached lorebook's cast at create_; `field` names a boolean field of the genre that switches it (absent = always for this genre). Validated by `genre()` (the field must exist and be boolean). The packager/announce carry it like `lorebook`.
- **SDK** `create-session` params gain `persona?: { name: string; description?: string }` (≤ 80 / ≤ 2,000 chars): the player the start form offers as a NEW persona; the person may pick an existing one instead; Start creates the card (the person's own, owned by them) and attaches it.
- **Server** `sessions:create` ONLY (at create, as the ruling says; never on update/attach — a later ★ if wanted, saying how removed members count): when the genre declares `castFromLorebook` and its switch field is true (or absent) and a book is attached, core seats the book's cast members as `sessionCharacters` — **never a persona card, and for a provided book only its provided-marked cards** (`ProvidedCharacterMark`) — the ownership check skipped for exactly those rows (the book must be readable by the person: `lorebookAccessFor` ≠ none); the person's own picks still pass today's check. **The binding check (`runLorebookBindingCheck`) adds no members to a provided book for anyone, the enabling admin included** (today it would mint the admin's new persona into the pub-wide book and seat it in everyone's later games). Nothing happens for a genre without the declaration. No ledger row (the confirm line is the cause shown).
- **Client** `answerCreateSession` validates `persona`; the start form's **Who you play as** is prefilled with the new persona (name, description) marked _new, from the game_; the one-press confirm shows _You play as: <name> (new)_ and _Who is in it: the world's own people_ when the switch field is on; Start creates the persona card through the existing `characters:create` door (persona flag) as the person — the reply guarded the way `awaitingCreate` does and matched by name, not the user's broadcast — and the draft then **becomes** that persona (kept as the person's own card; a retry after a refused session reuses it, the refusal names the card); then the session is created with it in `personaIds`. The draft takes precedence over the person's default persona (`preselectDefault`). _Change…_ lets the person pick an existing persona instead (the draft is dropped). The confirm's line is a variant of the existing _Playing as …_ fact: _Playing as <name> (new)_. The session's placeholder name is built on the client from the draft persona's name and the book's name (copy ★).
- **Port** (`mundi-M3b-S`): the lifesim/scenario shapes declare `castFromLorebook: { field: 'examples' }`; the wizard's Start sends `persona: { name, description }` (name from the Name field or the persona's; description from background + looks + traits as one short paragraph); the create step keeps reading the attached persona as the player; re-package 0.2.0 (unreleased).
- **Challenger round 1 (2026-11-05):** (1) admin personas minted into the provided book and seated everywhere — **accept**: provided-marked cards only, never personas, no binding rows into a provided book for anyone · (2) seating on update/attach was never asked — **accept**: create only · (3) stray/duplicate personas and the default persona competing — **accept**: the draft becomes the card, retry reuses, draft wins · (4) no session-change ledger row — **accept**, dropped · (5) the session name is built on the client — **accept**: m3b-C builds the placeholder (copy ★). The persona description's content (background + looks + traits) is the planner's reading — **copy ★ for the owner** with m3b-C's lines.
Lanes: **m3b-D** (SDK + server) and **m3b-C** (client + docs) first; **mundi-M3b-S** after D's name lands. Challenger before any lane. Gate: the launcher walk on a fresh pub and as a member: New game → confirm shows the new persona and the world's people → Start → the map, no in-session wizard.
- **M3b status (2026-11-05) — BUILT, reviewed, gate walked; ★ open.** Reports `plans/REPORT-m3b-D-2026-11-05.md`, `plans/REPORT-m3b-C-2026-11-05.md` (lead: one scope round, two fixes, then the gate-walk fix below), the port's in `~/.claude/plans/briefs/mundi-M3b-S-REPORT.md` (scope review: no objection; zip 0.2.0 sha256 `67004589…`). Hair: Red. Eyes: Green. Proficiency: Smart. Personality: Calm, Curious._), the map, no in-session wizard. **Found on the walk and fixed by the lead:** the game began as a newcomer with no traits — the session's stored `genreFields` held only `home`, because `sentGenreFields()` takes every preset ask the person did not touch OUT and the applet's prefill never counted as touching; the applet-prefill apply step now sets each prefilled ask as the person's answer (`StartSessionForm.svelte`, + a dom test with `presetAsks`); re-walked on a plain instance: the stored fields complete, the game's Profile shows the traits, money follows the origin ($1,200). Verification: `tsc` 4 errors, all in other programmes' test files (`CompileHistoryEntryModal.dom`, `arrangeCanvas.dom`, `unfiledScenesByEntry.int` ×2); targeted vitest 26 files / 283 (`sessions.castFromLorebook.int`, `castBindings.int`, `ownBookCreate.int`, `providedBooksPubWide.int`, `sessionForms`, `navigation.test`) and `sessionForms` 21 / 237 after the fix; svelte-check 0 errors / 8 pre-existing warnings; `npm run build` clean (exit 0).
  **Noticed, not done (core):** the one-press confirm prints object fields as raw JSON (_Your character: {"age":24,…}_, _Home: {"zone":…}_) · Change… to another preset resets the prefilled asks (they drop again on Start) · `characters:create:error` without a request id (C's report) · a **no-watch test instance** freezes the shell's `page.url` reactivity (blank page after Leave) — a harness artefact, absent on a plain `vite dev`; recorded in memory, no product change. **Port, Noticed:** `stepWorld` logs `Cannot read properties of null (reading 'id')` once while the world rows load; the tour's Skip is not remembered across launcher loads.

## 24. Owner rulings 2026-11-05 (morning) on the open ★ — and the lanes that build them

**Rulings (quoted; each supersedes or extends as marked):**
1. **★s22-C-1 → R32 amended for rulesets:** "Rulesets can't be locked, but incompatible rulesets can be locked out" + "Always lock out Incompatible, no setting". A preset's ruleset is a **default** (the pre-chosen row), never a lock; the server stops enforcing `atStart.locked` for `ruleset`, and the preset editor drops the lock for Rules. R32 (2026-10-20, "each layer sets a value and locks it or leaves it open") stands for every other setting. **Incompatible is always locked out** for a genre: a ruleset, a book on such rules, or a starter on such rules is greyed with its reason and refused by the server (extends today's disabled Incompatible blank rows; supersedes ★C-7 built tonight, which was unruled). "Genres expect, never lock" (2026-10-23) stands for every rating above Incompatible; `only` stays the one declared lock (★§22-2).
2. **★M3b-W-1 → yes:** values land in the session's own notes, reads check the notes first (the overlay carries attribute values: a member's write for the book's own people, or for the world, lands on the own book's row; reads own book, then attached).
3. **★M3b-W-2 → check the writer first, then create** the own-book row (the writer's `userId` into `worldOwnerOf`).
4. **★M3b-W-3 → the session's own notes are exempt from the write mode** (as lore already is).
5. **★M3b-W-4 → a player may hand-edit their own invited cast only** (their persona and the characters they brought; never the owner's seated cards or the world's people), **only attributes their ruleset marks player-editable** ("level shouldn't be but mood is"), within the genre's switch. **Attributes declare it** — a new ruleset-attribute field (🚧 name per §24; SDK; **default owner-only**, P1), shown in the ruleset editor and as a lock on the sheet.
6. **Honour mode split (supersedes the 2026-10-16 Crawler ruling "Sheet edits: disabled by default for players; honour mode toggles them on"):** two switches. **Honour mode** = keep every turn (no deletes/takebacks), as today. New 🚧 **Players edit their sheets** (purpose: "Players may change what their rules let them on their own characters"), **default ON in every genre with sheets, the Dungeon Crawler defaults OFF**.
7. **★M3b-S3-1 → manual** (no autoConvert).
8. **Ready-made group kept**; the **book list row's fit line always shows, as a third line** (STYLE-GUIDE §6.4 amended for book rows).
9. **Names:** the plugin stays **Mundi for Serene Pub**; the genre and the ruleset are **Mundi** (the ruleset "Mundi life" → "Mundi").
10. **Provided books re-import when the shipped book's content hash changes**, not only on a version change.
11. **Mundi's two books declare they use the ruleset** (`primaryRuleset: mundi:ruleset/life`).

**Lanes** (all `lane-supervisor` on Opus, Sonnet workers; challenger first on L, V2, E — they touch rulings):
- **s22-L** (client + server, start form + presets): rulings 1 and 8.
- **m3b-V2** (server state): rulings 2–4.
- **ed** (SDK + server + client): rulings 5–6; after m3b-V2 (both edit `state/write.ts`).
- **pb** (server plugins): ruling 10.
- **mundi-M3b-S5** (port): rulings 9, 11; after **ed** lands its SDK name, a follow-up marks Mundi's player-editable attributes (Mood, Hunger, Thirst, Energy, Hygiene ★ list for the owner).
- **§24.11 follow-up (2026-11-05):** lane mundi-M3b-S5 built ruling 9 (ruleset named _Mundi_; zip sha `0a92a872…`) but not 11 — a lorebook **file** has no field for its rules (the importer `importLorebookDocument`, `sockets/lorebooks.ts` ~4418, reads name/description/uuid, `extraJson`, `extensions.serenepub.storyTime`; the exporter writes no rules; the SDK's `lorebookFile.ts` has none), so every exported non-Freeform book comes back Freeform. **Owner ruling ★M3b-S5-1: "In the book file, beside the story clock."** Lane **bf** (core + SDK): the file carries the book's ruleset (+ version) beside `storyTime`; import reads it with `lorebooks:create`'s checks (unknown → Freeform + a warning); export writes it; the SDK file validator checks it. Then a port lane writes Mundi into both books, and lane **pb**'s re-import-on-change carries it to existing pubs.
- **Challenger round on §24 (2026-11-05) — five objections, all taken; the lead had described Honour mode backwards to the owner and corrected it before any build.** Today Honour mode **ON** is the permissive setting ("Lets players edit their own sheets by hand, and the host delete the latest turn where deleting is on. Off: neither." — `core-catalog genres.ts` `HONOR_MODE_FIELD`; `messages/verbs.ts` `turnDeleteVerdict`, refusal _Honour mode is off: turns can only be retaken…_). **Owner rulings after the correction (2026-11-05):**
  - **Honour mode flipped:** **ON = keep every turn** (no delete of the latest turn); its sheet half moves to the new **Players edit their sheets** (default ON; the Dungeon Crawler OFF). Back/Forward and the retake are untouched (10-23, 10-24). Stored data: a boot pass maps each stored `honorMode` — on sessions and on preset settings — `true` (today: deletes + sheet edits allowed) → Honour mode **false** + sheets **true**; `false` → Honour mode **true** + sheets **false**; absent → the genre's declared defaults (the Crawler declares Honour mode **ON**, so its default behaviour — every turn kept — is unchanged); a second boot changes nothing. The key's name is kept; its meaning is inverted (owner's call on a published SDK field, ruled).
  - **The per-attribute mark is the existing `changedBy`** (`rules · gm · holder · everyone`; editor + hand-edit door already built). **Missing `changedBy` stays "anyone"**; the core rulesets **mark the locked ones** (Level and the like → `rules`/`gm`) — a list for the owner. No new SDK field (R1).
  - **"Their own cast" = cards the player owns** (`characters.userId`): their persona and any seated card that is theirs; no new column.
  - **Values go to the session's own notes only when the session can't write its book** (a member on a pub-wide/shared book, or no book); on the owner's own book, values land on the book under its Full/Review/Off as today.
  - **s22-L decisions (lead, inside the ruling):** the preset's ruleset becomes the pre-chosen row (client) and the server's fallback when nothing valid is sent (it reached sessions only through the lock line); Incompatible is refused from one helper (`strictRulesRefusal` in `rulesets/bookRules.ts`, using `rulesetFit` where the genre declares `uses`) at create (kit, book, blank pick — refused, not swapped) and both attach paths (`sessions:update`, summarize); **a next chapter and any running session are never re-judged** (0.6 is in users' hands). No core genre rates anything Incompatible today — tests use a fixture genre.
  - **pb re-scoped:** the hash trigger already exists (`syncOne`, the `sha256` in the mark); the gap is that `upgrade` diffs only entries — it now carries stats, cast, bindings and the book's rules (bf's `importedBookRules`), and surfaces import warnings.
- **bf BUILT (2026-11-05):** `extensions.serenepub.primaryRuleset` (+ optional `rulesetVersion`) in the lorebook file; import reads it with `lorebooks:create`'s check (`chosenBookRules`), unknown → Freeform + a warning; export writes it for a non-Freeform book (Freeform byte-identical); SDK validator; 37 int tests + `lorebookFile.test` 5/5; sdk-tests no new failure. ★bf-1 an overwrite from a file with no field keeps the book's rules · ★bf-2 an unknown version keeps the ruleset unpinned + a warning · ★bf-3 the warnings' words (name the ruleset by id).
- **Owner rulings 2026-11-05 (late morning) — who may hand-edit, and the ruleset marks:**
  - **Ruleset marks (`changedBy`, missing = anyone):** **Vis Itineris — lock everything but Mood**; **Wherewithal — lock Nerve, Favour, Provisions; Mood free**; **Mundi — Mood and Age free, the rest locked** (Life, Energy, Hunger, Thirst, Hygiene, Money, Bond, Romance, Romance status, Approval, Met, Occupation, Home).

I.e. session owner can edit any and override restrictions, guests can edit only defaults, for example." This **supersedes the single "Players edit their sheets" switch** ruled earlier today; the four levels the owner named (as the rules say · no editing · all on own cards · all on anyone's) are the vocabulary a genre composes per role (owner, guests/players). The Honour mode flip stands (ON = keep every turn); its sheet half goes into these controls.
